# Spectra launch verification report

Started: 8 November 2026. Closed: 10 November 2026 (Madrid), at Pablo's explicit request to finish verification. Post-LV-61 whole/static/live checks pass. Shared cloud and own desktop replicas/payloads now remain visible behind other accounts' older rows, without reading their discarded projections. The bounded correction's extra indexed range and index maintenance are disclosed. Native approvals, first-attempt physical OpenRouter/Codex writes and exact-byte Undo remain verified from LV-59/60. Historical model/runtime, partial UI-matrix coverage and strict aggregate-cost observations remain documented limits of the evidence; this report does not promise error-free behavior.

## Fixed

### LV-61 — foreign replica density hides available work and payloads — corrected and verified

The shared work metadata, Library presentation and payload readers bounded all accounts' replicas to 20 before checking local ownership. Four actual-handler cases fail before production with 80 neighbors passing (replica-visible-61-before-final.log): 100 older foreign local rows hide the shared cloud/own local pair, empty Library status and return null for both cloud and local-latest payload preferences. With only one foreign row, the same valid pair remains visible. These are isolated synthetic fixtures using a real test device/mount/stored payload; no foreign user's desktop data is accessed.

Caller/consequence audit precedes production in the LV-61 ledger below. The correction at convex/replicas.ts:431 adds one nine-line visibleReplicasForWork routine inside the existing file, reused at lines 451, 590 and 750. It reads the canonical cloud through the existing work/kind index and only this account's local copies through by_work_kind_account (convex/schema.ts:2634), reading at most 20 replica documents combined and retaining chronological presentation order. Shared cloud visibility is preserved even when its registered account differs; local filesystem paths stay private. Artifact capabilities, live-device presentation, conflict/write guards, protected pending selection and verified payload handling are unchanged. Producer/global sweep/migration ranges retain their existing behavior; this is a reader-density correction, not a new claim about unlimited same-account devices/copies.

All four density cases now return identical correct results at one and 100 foreign rows, with constant full-handler calls/replica bytes and zero writes. The replica ranges return exactly two documents in work/payload readers; Library returns three including its unchanged own-attention row. Two additional cloud-present/absent cases preserve both the 20-row DB/API ceiling and chronological order. No post-index filter is used. Ten existing test files / 280 tests pass, covering replica properties, native materialization/commits, Bridge sync, authorization, DOCX/Office and restore neighbors (replica-visible-61-focused-final.log). The correction adds one indexed DB range per affected reader and one index entry's storage/maintenance per replica write; ordinary visible rows/bytes are unchanged, while foreign projections are excluded. No new external function call, idle timer/subscription, UI or file is added. These counts are API returned rows/serialized JSON, not engine scans, physical I/O or billing.

Local index/functions deploy in 1.73 s (replica-visible-61-deploy.log). Native Word before/reload-after remains identical at 118 words/Saved; original-profile Bridge is automatically connected, retained cloud work opens and stays Saved for 77.476 seconds without any artifact/version/queried-sync write, and second-context Vectors stays Saved/275 words. All ten native-61/final-61 screenshots were opened and inspected. Original Word/Vectors/task snapshots, both physical hashes, all preference layers and the rebuilt Bridge binary match; no native picker/reset/reconfiguration, AI request or stack restart is needed. The terminal whole gate passes 665 files / 5,748 tests in 340.93 s, one existing skip, plus both TypeScript projects, zero-warning lint and whitespace. All 175 source/config hashes/set match before/after; Rust is unchanged. Evidence: final-61-integrity.json, source-61-{before,after}.json, final-61-* logs/JSON/images. No commit, push or PR.

### LV-60 — answered approval remains actionable during the resumed write — corrected and verified

AI-099-allow-1.png and AI-100-allow-{1,5}.png reproduce Allowed in the live trace alongside a separate Needs your OK card with enabled Allow/Don't allow controls. LV-59 fixes terminal convergence; these controls persist throughout the resumed work. Source audit finds spectraHarness.answer patches the harness answer/run to queued but leaves the canonical spectraResultCards row unchanged. answerAndResume then reads that stale row through spectraRuns:get and streams it back as result_card; claimExecution updates the run but not its card. The normal legacy claimConfirmation path already clears its card actions, which explains the harness-only inconsistency. Duplicate submission is server-fenced; duplicate execution is not demonstrated.

Before editing, searched every answer/confirm/revise caller, both approval and question branches, replay/ownership/execution guards, run/card/tab readers and projection triggers. The smallest consistent correction needs the existing projection-aware mutation builder for this answer only, plus clearing this run's card actions/status atomically after a validated answer. Other harness mutations retain their original builder. This is more than a one-line UI change because canonical cards and their compact summaries must agree across browsers; no component/CSS/copy filter is proposed. The existing by_run index scopes card reads, and existing triggers maintain compact card/tab/analytics rows. Additional event-driven read-model work is a correctness cost to measure, not an idle-efficiency saving. Three real-handler approval/rejection/question regressions fail before production, while eleven existing cases pass (answered-card-60-before.log). At both 1 and 100 unrelated same-account runs/cards, the answered canonical card and compact summary retain their awaiting status/actions before any new worker starts. Replay remains fenced; no duplicate execution is asserted. Preserve every approval scope and native safety guard.

Expanded live/focused verification: 152 tests pass and AI-101 Codex Luna native write/exact-byte Undo passes, including zero Allow buttons at 1.161 and 5.880 seconds after answering. The first full gate reports 5,741 passing tests and one command-parity failure: its source parser recognizes only literal mutation/action names and falsely reports the actual exported projectedMutation answer as missing. Actual deployed RPC and real-handler tests succeed. Before updating this test helper, audited its public-write catalog/coverage consumers and the two builder-export modules. Resolve named mutation/action import aliases from those same modules, so the existing guard discovers the actual function rather than excluding it or changing product behavior to fit a regex. Other assertions and timeouts remain unchanged; rerun parity, focused neighbors and the full gate.

Correction at convex/spectraHarness.ts:363: only answer uses the existing projection-aware builder; other harness functions retain their original builder. After all answer/approval-scope validation and the queued-run patch, its local resumeCards routine uses the existing by_run index and sets this run’s canonical cards to queued with empty actions. Existing triggers update compact card/tab/analytics state in the same transaction. Titles, work links and provenance are preserved, and no UI component, CSS, copy, schema/index or native safety rule changes. The small routine is shared by accepted/rejected approvals and answered questions; replay returns before it runs. This adds a scoped card collection and per-card patch plus existing compact read-model maintenance per validated answer. It is necessary consistency work, not a cost saving; it adds no idle workload.

All three new real-handler cases now pass at 1/100 unrelated same-account runs/cards. Canonical and compact cards are queued with no actions before another worker starts; the compact run summary is queued too. Every unrelated card retains its awaiting state/actions. Full handler read-call and write counts are identical across densities; the canonical-card by_run range returns exactly one row without a post-index filter. A repeated answer performs zero writes. Temporary measurement logging was removed before the final gate; no physical-I/O/billing saving is inferred. Nine focused files / 160 tests pass, covering parity, approval scopes, native commits, projection heartbeat and both hook/route neighbors (answered-card-60b-focused.log).

Local deployment succeeds in 1.62 s (answered-card-60-deploy.log). AI-101 repeats the exact native summary request through Codex GPT-6-Luna; both main and title stay on Codex, without fallback or failed-tool retry. The first-try physical DOCX gains exactly one correct summary, 118→142 words; all original XML children survive and only word/document.xml changes. During resumed work at 1.161 and 5.880 seconds, the thread has zero Allow buttons and displays Allowed; no duplicate approval card returns at completion or Undo. Active harness time is 8.988 + 7.263 = 16.251 s, excluding the operator’s approval wait. Physical write SHA is 8A5DFED1158A40B60B0789E5B528468E4C7D87BE335DC2DA7A1127C6CC2807A4; UI Undo restores the exact baseline 3235F7E3…E6FFC3 with no ZIP-entry differences. All 22 ai-101 captures were opened and inspected; JSON state, provider/trace summary and word-after/word-undo retain the evidence. Re-adding the same file reused canonical work z97epvgr07m133m3hfaek90dgs8hzctr; no duplicate work was made.

Final cleanup restores all four baseline preference layers, stops only this test selection through the web, preserves physical files/test work and leaves the original Bridge connected. Retained test work stays Saved/118 words for 162.081 seconds without changing artifact metadata, versions or queried sync state. Original native Word/Vectors/task snapshots and both physical hashes remain exact. Home and second-context Saved/275-word Vectors have zero pageerrors/Stop residue, with nine final-60 images inspected. The authoritative final-60b gate passes 665 files / 5,742 tests, one existing file/test skipped, 318.03 s, terminal exit 0, plus both TypeScript projects, zero-warning lint and whitespace. All 175 code/config hashes/set match across the gate; only spectraHarness.ts, its existing test and the parity test differ from LV-59. Rust source remains unchanged. The earlier gate’s sole parser failure is retained in final-60-vitest.log and superseded, not hidden. No Bridge reset/profile change, stack restart, commit, push or PR.

### LV-59 — accepted native approval remains after successful write/Undo — corrected and verified

AI-097's targeted OpenRouter / Medium run writes the physical Word file on its first attempt (verified request nn74vhr0vn0nd2mgmamcp71wmx8hy8er, outcome native_verified) and UI Undo restores its exact original SHA-256. However ai-097-68-saved.png and -69-undone.png show the accepted approval returning in the island/thread after completion. Stored approval is accepted, operation executed, result card completed and harness has no pending input; reopening the thread clears the stale UI (-74-thread-reopened.png). No second approval is granted.

Pre-edit caller/consequence audit: useSpectraRun is shared by shell, chat, thread, island, agent and landing callers. The answerAndResume prelude emits result_card, whereas new/retry runs emit run_created. consume bound its local shared stream only on run_created, so resumed replies were not published to the other page engines and a paused owner could retain its old approval. Audited both prelude routes, request cleanup/durable handoff, all local-run-stream publishers/listeners and shared hook callers. The one-line correction at src/features/spectra/ai/use-spectra-run.ts:979 also binds the acknowledged result_card run ID. Three real two-hook approval/rejection/question regressions verify the original engine clears and receives the final cards, with zero extra getSpectraRunEvents calls instead of one. No extra query, timer, subscription, server write, permission relaxation or UI/copy change.

The valid regression fails before source and passes afterwards; six files / 93 focused tests pass. An initial fixture had invalid input_answered fields; those were removed and valid before/after runs captured separately (approval-stream-59-valid-{before,after}.log). AI-098 then proves native write/exact-byte Undo again and clears the island question, but its thread still retains the actionable card (-68/-69). DOM inspection places it outside the reply in the active card stack; stored run/card/read models are completed. This partial retest is recorded as a failure.

Follow-up root audit before expansion: scripts/run-relay.mjs buffers/replays by signed runId:executionId, and answerAndResume retains that execution. A new watcher can receive the previous input_required/end before its new scheduled publisher starts. consume stops at that old question and misses the new final. Audited dispatch, payloadFrom, scheduled callback, runRelayInvite/openRunRelay, resumePayload/handoff, signing/auth/replay/bounds and every caller. At src/app/api/spectra/run/runtime.ts:160 each user dispatch now creates a relayId in its existing scheduled payload/invite; payloadFrom passes it into the worker. src/app/api/spectra/run/harness/attempt.ts:132 preserves it across automatic worker handoffs. src/features/spectra/ai/harness/run-relay.ts:17 signs both watcher and publisher for that same dispatch identity. Legacy jobs without the optional identity retain the old channel. Four existing production files and three existing test files change; no Convex/schema, gateway or Rust edit.

Both relay-identity/handoff regressions fail before production (11 existing cases pass), then eight files / 112 tests pass including real relay auth/replay/handoff neighbors (relay-session-59-{before,focused}.log). One UUID adds about 50 serialized bytes to the existing job payload and no DB call or extra connection. Old and resumed channel buffers may coexist until the existing two-minute idle cleanup; each retains its existing bounded 2 MiB window and signed expiry. That bounded transient memory/storage tradeoff is explicit, rather than claiming zero overhead.

Final-code retests AI-099 (OpenRouter openai/gpt-6-luna / Amazon Bedrock) and AI-100 (Codex GPT-6-Luna, including title) pass. Each exact prompt appends one correct summary on the first read/edit attempt; original DOCX XML children remain intact and only word/document.xml changes. Physical 118-word fixture becomes 152/142 words respectively, then UI Undo restores SHA-256 3235F7E33A866D1073BB895CBA2C554CD0CA6BB4DBBDFB30B0657606C1E6FFC3 and every ZIP entry exactly. Terminal thread/island show Allowed/completed with no pending approval or Stop, and larger artifact cards remain. All ai-099/100-{before,working,allow,66-completed,67-document-after,68-saved,69-undone} captures and their actual numbered variants were inspected. During the resumed work, an old actionable card is briefly visible alongside Allowed; it clears at completion. That separate transient-control finding remains below; this fix establishes terminal convergence, not immediate removal throughout the write.

AI-099 active harness time is 4.242 + 4.073 = 8.315 s; AI-100 is 14.249 + 7.984 = 22.233 s. These exclude the operator's approval wait. Trace elapsed values reset on resume and are not end-to-end totals. Both have successful read_artifact/edit_artifact, no failed tool retry or runtime fallback; ensureApproved's controlled pause is expected. Thus this Codex sample's longer model turn is not an edit retry; different provider/load/cache conditions do not establish a general benchmark. Per-run trace-summary, word-after/word-undo and native state JSON retain the evidence.

Cleanup: the web/native picker selected only the disposable Launch verification OpenRouter Word.docx on the already connected original Bridge profile. After all four runs, baseline High/Preferred/Spectra-default preferences are restored through the web and all four saved layers match exactly. Stop using this file removes only that test selection; physical files and clearly named cloud test work remain. Reopening it after stopping retains Saved/118 words for 216.454 seconds with exact artifact/version/queried-sync snapshots and no opening write. Original Word/Vectors/task snapshots and both physical Word hashes remain unchanged. Home, connected empty-selection Devices and second-context Saved/275-word Vectors pass with zero pageerrors or Stop residue; all nine final-59 screenshots were inspected.

The authoritative final-59b gate passes **665 files / 5,739 tests**, one existing file/test skipped, **337.75 s**, terminal exit 0, plus both TypeScript projects, zero-warning lint and whitespace. All 173 changed/untracked code/config hashes and their set match across the gate; exactly the seven audited files differ from LV-58 and Rust source is unchanged (final-59-integrity.json). ### LV-58 — native resolution loses exact write identity under unrelated history — corrected and verified

All 11 new real-handler cases fail before production, with 69 existing passes (write-identity-58-before.log). An exact pending/claimed request works with one newer failed request but is rejected behind 100 failures from another own copy. Foreign, other-work, inactive or missing explicit IDs silently substitute a different pending write. Verified/failed/conflicted completion, queued-write supersession and external-upload supersession leave the target conflict resolving behind 100 conflicts bound to a different actual queued copy under the SAME account. The old settlement range reads 50 unrelated rows rather than its own target.

At convex/replicas.ts:1367–1373, the provided request ID selects one point read, validated against both actor account and work; the no-ID compatibility path retains its bounded 50-row lookup and region matching. Pending/claimed receipt binding still covers the whole conflict bar. An explicit invalid/inactive receipt now uses the existing safe convergence/refusal checks instead of selecting a different pending file write. This intentional identity boundary is tested; ordinary valid-ID and legacy lookup each use one database call. Existing auth, work ownership, request-state and native write/readback guards stay intact.

At convex/bridgeSync.ts:1317, :1943, :1989 and convex/replicaMaterialization.ts:436, existing 50-row ranges now bind resolutionRequestId, accountId and resolving state before the cap. The narrow index at convex/schema.ts:2728 prevents both same-account other-request starvation and discarded history reads. Existing per-row work/request guards remain. Both Office queue→resolve routes, all resolving-conflict writers/readers, Bridge credentials/claim/readback, both materialization supersede call sites, external upload, payload-owner triggers and run settlement were audited before production. No extra query, helper, table, cache, timer, UI or native-safety relaxation. Each conflict row gains one additional index entry: storage and maintenance on writes are an explicit tradeoff. Global sweeps/migrations remain global.

All 11 cases and ten focused files / 303 tests pass (write-identity-58-focused.log). Exact-ID sparse/dense tests retain the requested pending/claimed copy, have identical whole-handler calls/documents/writes, and perform no request-history take. Four invalid-ID cases leave conflicts exact and cannot silently select another request. Five lifecycle scenarios preserve unrelated conflict rows and the actual other request; the four observed public Bridge handler scenarios have constant full read counts at 1/100 unrelated conflicts and one selected resolving-conflict range row without a post-index database filter. Queued-write supersession also passes both densities through the real signed admission path. Trigger/other handler reads are included in total-count comparisons; the selected range row is not described as total physical I/O. No aggregate billing or I/O percentage is inferred.

The new index and functions deploy successfully in 1.81 s (write-identity-58-deploy.log). Native Word reloads with identical 118-word text and Saved status; physical SHA, native/Vectors metadata, task content/eight versions and all four original preference layers remain exact (final-58-integrity.json). Home → original-profile automatically connected Devices → Home and second-context Saved Vectors pass with zero pageerrors/Stop residue. All seven native-58-{01-home,02-before,03-after} and final-58-{home,devices,home-restored,artifact} images are opened and inspected. Density and completion scenarios use isolated real-handler fixtures, not a new live desktop write.

The terminal whole gate passes **665 files / 5,734 tests**, one existing file/test skipped, **308.99 s**, exit 0 (--maxWorkers=2). Both TypeScript projects, zero-warning lint and whitespace pass. All 169 code/config hashes and the exact set match across the gate; only the five audited source/test/schema files differ from LV-57. Rust source is unchanged. No AI request, native reconfiguration, stack restart, commit, push or PR.

### LV-57 — conflict writers cross account boundaries and starve own settlement — corrected and verified

Six actual-handler regressions fail before production, with 86 neighboring passes (conflict-writes-57-all-before.log). Keep local and Keep Spectra resolve 20 foreign rows while the own conflict after 100 peer rows remains open. A foreign-only conflict blocks protected-original apply. The durable native commit successfully applies the Word content but leaves its own conflict open beyond the foreign rows.

Five existing ranges at convex/replicas.ts:1010, :1230, :1291 and convex/nativeReconciliation.ts:142, :283 now include the acting account before revision matching/first or take(20). They reuse LV-56's by_work_account_state index. Existing auth, work ownership, native receipts, own conflict guard, returned shapes and limits stay intact. No new query, index, schema, subscription, timer, UI/copy or native-safety relaxation. Missing own rows are now created as required; ordinary own-only paths preserve their original read/write structure. Global migration/sweep behavior is unchanged.

All six regressions and seven focused files / 220 tests pass. Tests verify exact foreign rows remain unchanged, an own preview contains only the supplied own words, replay creates no extra conflict, and protected apply still refuses an own open conflict before allowing the foreign-only case to queue exclusively to the own device/replica. Both resolutions retain one own range row at either 1 or 100 peer conflicts and exactly two syncConflicts documents including the existing trigger get; total database calls and writes match between the two densities. The first measurement expectation counted only the query; the actual observer exposed the trigger get, and the test now includes it rather than hiding that cost. No production workaround was added for that test expectation. Durable native settlement preserves the expected canonical content and leaves foreign rows exact. No aggregate physical-I/O/billing saving is inferred.

Local deployment reports functions ready (conflict-writes-57-deploy.log). Native Word before/reload-after has identical 118-word text and Saved status; the after capture follows functions-ready. Home → automatically connected original-profile Bridge → Home and second-context Saved Vectors pass with zero pageerrors/Stop residue. All seven native-57-{01-home,02-before,03-after} and final-57-{home,devices,home-restored,artifact} images are opened and inspected. ### LV-05 retained-work subcase: native account/deployment stores — correction verified in disposable servers

- Original protected HTTP fixture exposed both a first-account work and a null-owner work after unpairing and pairing a different device (`store-repair-before.log`, both hidden checks false). Production used one lazy global `rooms::shared()` store across browser HTTP, browser/server rooms, local store sockets and hosted pipes; its optional projection owner was not an authorization boundary.
- All four lookups now select the verified account/deployment's private store through `ServerState::prepare_work_rooms` (`bridge/bridge-core/src/server.rs`). Pair/resume/health use their existing authenticated response's owner; startup binds its cached owner to device, deployment and credential hash. First migration waits until the app owns its listener. Legacy work is copied read-only only after bounded cloud ownership proof, including null-owner rows, exact state/update/history and existing store id. Failure leaves the store unavailable, never an empty published room; source files remain unchanged, retries preserve newer scoped work, and backup coverage resets. The profile's target directory stays separate from the actual old legacy source.
- `Rooms::for_account` and scoped restore wait for every ownership batch before copying. A 201-id HTTP ownership fixture proves 100/100/1 batches, refusal on a mismatched second batch with zero copied work/no completion marker, successful retry of 101 owned rows, zero warm-reopen queries, and exclusion of foreign work from a mixed backup (`account-rooms-migration-network.log`, covered again in the final suite). A validated compressed bundle avoids a redundant third decompression pass; scoped restore and unscoped restore use validation/import passes without retaining the full decompressed backup in memory.
- Account A→B→A keeps one Rooms map per account so old close/compaction cannot race a second room map for the same account. Unpairing clears active scope/restore credentials and revokes local/hosted store, room and CLI channels, including pending HTTP restore authorization. A late migration cannot publish its old account, and a cancelled pairing cannot publish its browser session. Hosted tickets now return the already-read device owner; a wrong-owner ticket is refused before dialing. Local Node preflight additionally matches the native account/deployment to the authenticated point lookup, preventing copied-deployment device ids from authorizing another store. No extra ordinary editing query, subscription or cloud health read was added.
- The corrected external HTTP fixture passes with explicit verified store preparation at pairing: both hidden checks are true, and the original owner recovers both works after switching back (`store-repair-after.log`). It uses a new absolute disposable profile/random port and never prepares the installed relay token or runs a CLI. The final 148 native tests include real ephemeral HTTP/WebSocket migration, A→B→A, late response, pending restore and hosted three-pipe revocation fixtures; app test compilation passes. The hosted CLI fixture sends no start frame; a separate dummy-task test verifies child cancellation, without claiming a real CLI run.
- JS/Convex verification: 658 files / 5,592 tests pass with two workers and unchanged timeouts (`account-binding-vitest-bounded-final.log`); app/Convex TypeScript, lint and whitespace pass (`account-binding-tsc-{app,convex}.log`, `account-binding-lint.log`, `account-binding-diff-check.log`). The first unrestricted full run timed out in three files while other heavy checks ran (two tests and one Chromium close hook); all 31 tests in those files pass separately (`account-binding-timeout-recheck.log`), and the complete bounded run passes without relaxing assertions/timeouts. The updated cloud ticket response is deployed to instance 8 (`account-binding-cloud-deploy.log`).
- Scope limit: this verifies the native retained-work correction with disposable servers, not a rebuilt installed Bridge or the complete browser/native account matrix. The raw-token replay probe did not exercise the real app cleanup; that overstatement is corrected in Evidence. Reproduced browser continuation/cache failures are fixed in the separate subcase above. The installed real-account Bridge, keyring and data were untouched. Overall LV-05 still needs the live isolated checks.

### LV-03: conversational Undo guessed an inverse — fixed for available live coverage

- **LV-03: conversational Undo does not restore the prior state.** After AI-005 changed Q3 from `5.0 Hz × 0.80 m = 4.0 m/s` to a multi-step `1.25 m` question, `Undo that.` (AI-006) invented a different one-step question (`3.0/4.0 = 0.75 m`) instead of restoring the original. Trace `yh72aq1m8bvddwc2r6fv8z137x8hvp7z` shows reads and `edit_artifact`, no history restore. Screenshots `ai-006-undo-{before,working,finished}.png` inspected. All tools report success, so tool success alone would miss this defect.
  - Root-path audit: the full reversal exists in `/api/spectra/run` action `revert`, including native artifact snapshots, new works, calendar, Library, settings, and memory. However `commands/ai-reach.ts` explicitly classifies `conversation.review_ai_edit` as user-only; the harness exposes per-work history restore through command discovery, not whole-run reversal. Prior thread replies do not contain the original full artifact content. The model therefore guessed an earlier question.
  - This needs more than a one-line correctness fix: expose or route an explicitly requested prior-run reversal through the existing scoped reversal path, preserving its conflict and device fences. A prompt that merely asks the model to approximate an inverse edit would not satisfy the all-lasting-changes requirement. The following audits were recorded before that larger implementation.
  - UI Undo for AI-006 passed: restored the exact AI-005 question, answer 1.25, and explanation, then marked the run Undone. `ai-006-ui-undo.png` inspected. The defect is the conversational route, not that successful reversal path.

- **LV-03 first live tool retest exposed a completion-contract gap:** AI-027 successfully edited the test quiz and created a labelled task/calendar event (18 s UI). AI-028 selected `undo_previous_run`, executed reversal successfully, then failed in `spectraRuns:updateStatus`: `A run without a committed operation or artifact version cannot be marked as saved.` Trace `yh7e318vm21xj06vrn0ka574e98hvb9b.jsonl` proves this is final settlement, not tool selection or model failure; before/working/finished screenshots for both runs were inspected. Pre-edit consequence audit: keep that saved-outcome guard intact. Add one Undo-only receipt mutation that validates current/prior ownership, school, thread, attempt, terminal status and the prior card's Undone marker, then records an executed `run.revert` operation for the requesting run. This supplies durable truthful evidence for existing completion, retry and partial-work handling without adding ordinary-run reads/writes or claiming that reversal supports redo. Test the actual updateStatus guard with this receipt before another live run.
  The observed post-effect failure also requires safe recovery: attribute the prior card's Undone marker to the requesting run, and allow only that same requester to recover its completion receipt without repeating snapshot/command writes. Direct repeated button Undo and unrelated requesting runs still stop before writing; test both paths. Existing card provenance is preserved.

- **LV-03 additional reversal fences (pre-edit audit):** the extracted existing UI reversal returns 200 for an old execution id and reaches `reverseWrites` before the artifact writer rejects stale work (`revert-shared-before.log`, 4 passed / 1 failed). Check the authenticated run's execution before planning or writing. Also reject a card already marked Undone before snapshot restoration, so repeating a completed Undo cannot overwrite later changes; these checks affect only explicit reversal calls and add no database operations. The exact-snapshot, later-human-conflict, created-work and native-version tests cover the shared UI path.

- **LV-03 tool/approval consequence audit:** searched the harness backend, tool registry, command reach catalog, loop scheduling, attempt outcomes, saved approvals and every reversal caller. Add a no-argument previous-run tool, an on-demand indexed predecessor query and an exclusive-call scheduling flag; ordinary calls retain existing parallelism. Before sharing the human reversal, check every sensitive category against the already-loaded permission preferences, count local replicas only during Undo, and bind any approval to the prior run/attempt in the durable pending input. Preserve Never choices, native/history conflict checks and the existing direct button; the Undo reply must not advertise an unsupported redo. Add the execution fence inside `reverseWrites` as well, so a changed attempt is rejected transactionally before command receipts are reversed. This adds no ordinary-run database operations or new subscriptions.

- **LV-03 implementation scope audit (before any Undo code edit):** the model currently has version restoration but no deterministic prior-run reversal tool. The existing route already reverses artifact snapshots, created works and non-artifact receipts, with native/device conflicts. A correct fix needs to share that exact orchestrator, select only the preceding run using the existing thread index with ownership/execution checks, and expose a no-target-argument harness tool across all three runtimes. It must retain permission/approval checks for native files and sensitive reversed operations, serialize whole-run Undo against other writes, and avoid replaying an already undone run. This is larger than a few lines because a prompt-only inverse edit, an unscoped raw reverseWrites tool, or treating tool success as complete reversal would leave the reproduced defect or bypass its fences. No ordinary-run database read/write should be added; preceding-run lookup is on demand, bounded and metadata-only. The shared UI Undo path and focused artifact/command/native conflict tests must pass before live conversational retesting. The implementation was subsequently verified below; this paragraph retains the pre-edit scope audit.


- Change: share the existing route reversal in `harness/revert-run.ts`; add `undo_previous_run` through the common runtime tool registry, the bounded `spectraRuns:previousForUndo` lookup, approval-scope and exclusive-call handling, transaction execution fences and the idempotent `recordReversal` receipt. Prompt guidance selects this saved-state path. No ordinary-run database read/write or subscription was added.
- Verification: original stale/repeated reversal regressions failed before the fences and pass afterward. Exact snapshot, later human conflict, created-work/native-version paths, permission denial/approval, prior-run ownership/attempt/thread lookup, ordinary parallelism, exclusive scheduling and completion-receipt/recovery checks pass. Latest focused run: 4 files / 31 tests (`undo-recovery-final.log`); earlier combined loop/attempt/shared paths: 5 files / 47 tests, with expected mocked provider-failure logs (`undo-receipt-first.log`). Both TypeScript checks and lint pass. The complete suite passes: 655 files / 5,564 tests, one file/test skipped, 303.23 s (`vitest-undo-final.log`). The prompt-budget check initially failed; shortening only the new instruction restored its existing limit, and the focused prompt/loop checks also pass all 35 tests.
- Live AI-029/030: changed only Q3, created a labelled task and calendar event; then typed exactly `Undo that.`. Both runs completed (13.39 s / 5.30 s total trace; UI 14 s / 6 s), no failed tool/Convex call. `undo-live-proof-030.json` proves exact title/summary/tags/content restoration; `undo-live-effects-030.json` proves the task's Library placement is trashed and the event is removed. Task records themselves remain active under the existing Library Trash model; a deleted primary placement is the intended reversible removal. Screenshots `ai-029-{before,working,finished}.png` and `ai-030-{before,working,finished}.png` all inspected. Run ids: `yh782nnqj12ee7m6y11cy97k198hvft6`, `yh735hgmt41w5d92yqgyxzrahs8hv13j`.
- Coverage update: common code and mocked native-version/permission checks pass; subsequent pure-Codex native reversal and exact-byte restoration pass under LV-33 / AI-048. Claude coverage is excluded by the latest explicit user direction. AI-027/028 retain the first failed-settlement evidence; their test quiz is also exactly restored, task placement trashed and calendar event removed (`undo-live-comparison.json`).
- Follow-up regression results: the owner-only replica counter rejected a modify collaborator, Stop during metadata preflight still began reversal, and native discovery truncated 30 targets to 24. Each failed before correction. Undo now uses an access-checked boolean for only the actor's own local replicas, checks Stop after the awaited query, and derives targets from affected artifact operations as well as the displayed cards/snapshots, batching native metadata discovery by 24. The policy counter/query size limit remain unchanged. Shared/permission/Stop plus replica suite: 2 files / 66 tests pass (`undo-shared-stop-after.log`); native discovery/shared orchestrator/backend: 3 files / 19 tests pass (`undo-native-bounds-after.log`). Both TypeScript checks passed after the shared query, which was deployed locally. The final server-helper bounds edit passes app TypeScript (`tsc-undo-bounds.log`), lint (`lint-undo-bounds.log`) and the complete suite: 656 files / 5,568 tests passed, one file/test skipped (`vitest-undo-bounds-final.log`). No additional paid native run was made for these metadata/fence corrections.
- Native-history follow-up: four focused regressions reproduced overwrite of a later manual title, intermediate restore selection beyond 50 rows and partial reversal when the earliest snapshot had fallen out of retention. The native plan now also compares titles, reads at most Bridge's existing 200 metadata rows, and keeps the work with an explanatory history note when that full window still lies inside the run. The direct History Restore path and retention policy are unchanged; no extra Convex request, ordinary-run work or content/state listing is added. All 38 tests across native planning, shared orchestration, backend Undo and History Restore pass (`undo-native-history-after.log`); app TypeScript and lint pass (`tsc-undo-history.log`, `lint-undo-history.log`). The complete suite passes 658 files / 5,584 tests, one file/test skipped, 313.22 s (`vitest-undo-history-final.log`). ### LV-02: concurrent task-note additions are lost — fixed, broader campaign ongoing

- Symptom and evidence: in two independent Andy contexts on the same task, each added a different paragraph at nearly the same time. Both contexts showed only `LAUNCHVERIFY-task-0-20261108` after reload; marker 1 vanished. Original notes survived. `manual-task-{0,1}-{before,after}.png`, `manual-task-07-reload-investigate.png`, `manual-task-08-second-context-reload.png`, `sheet-manual-task.png` were inspected.
- Root cause: `artifact-studio-value-utils.ts`, `mergeConcurrentText`, rejected two pure insertions at the same offset. Its save caller, `draftContentForLiveWrite`, uses the local value when the merge reports a collision, discarding the peer's addition.
- Change: three lines preserve both insertion strings in deterministic order. Overlapping replacements/deletions keep existing collision handling. No database operations were added.
- Regression evidence: the new focused task-note test failed before the fix with only Addition A retained (`task-merge-before.log`). After the fix, 51 tests across five directly affected files and 28 task/history/source tests passed (`task-merge-after.log`, `task-merge-neighbors.log`). Updated the existing same-offset text expectation to retain both insertions; competing replacement tests still pass.
- Browser verification: repeating the original two-context test with `task-fixed01` markers preserved both additions exactly once after reload in both contexts, with no offline banner. All six `manual-task-fixed01-*.png` screenshots were inspected in `sheet-manual-task-fixed01.png`.
- Consequence audit, recorded before editing, follows. Full-suite and remaining artifact checks are still pending.

LV-02 pre-edit analysis: traced `TaskStudio` -> `applySingleTask` -> `ArtifactStudio.synchronizeDraft` -> `draftContentForLiveWrite` -> `rebaseCollaborativeValue` -> `mergeConcurrentText`, plus the native structured Yjs adapter and optimistic region controller. Both inserts at the same character offset are currently treated as a collision; the save helper takes the local value and ignores that collision, allowing the peer's appended paragraph to be removed. The narrow proposed change preserves two pure insertions at one offset in a deterministic order while retaining collision handling for competing replacements/deletions. Searched all callers: the shared helper also serves incoming snapshots, structured editor save, undo/redo, and presentation/whiteboard gestures. Check those existing tests, same-offset insertions in both orders, and the original two-context task reproduction. Document/code native body bindings bypass this string-save path. No Convex reads, subscriptions, or schema changes are added.

## Found, not fixed


- AI-068 Codex resume stalls after its reasoning item starts, with no further model/tool output for 120 s. Stopped in the UI before automatic paid fallback; no writes or fallback occurred. The pause/resume protocol starts a fresh thread and passes the answered tool result; the trace does not establish a broken app-side cause. AI-073 resumes in 4.137 s and AI-079 in 34.283 s, both pure Luna, so an intermittent stall remains rather than a blanket resume failure.

- AI-080/084 synced-file identification is corrected through the existing title lookup under LV-42; AI-087 positively exercises orgTree:search and finds the exact fixture. AI-085/086 correct-looking answers through semantic search remain weaker evidence. Unopened desktop content is still intentionally absent from the content index; no body upload or broad scan was introduced.
- AI-081 successfully lists actual Physics folder names through seven read-only devices calls, with no file contents/commands/deletion. It omits the requested offer to sync. The safe capability is proved; this response detail remains a model omission.

- Codex AI-065 creates the correct task for a fresh, explicit progress document but omits its embed. The document stays unchanged. AI-063 alone was inconclusive because its fixture already had an old next-step task embed; AI-065 establishes the missing positive behavior.
- AI-066 recommended an elapsed 16:00–17:30 block at 19:21. LV-41 adds a future-time planning instruction; AI-075 explicitly recognizes that block has passed and creates a correct 45-minute task for the next available study time. This live case passes; no broad guarantee about model reasoning is inferred.

- AI-056 line/plane card says α = 90° − φ without defining φ as the acute angle with the normal; an obtuse vector/normal angle would yield a negative α. Its equivalent absolute-dot sine formula is correct. Model wording ambiguity is recorded; no historical content was silently corrected.
- AI-049 repeats the requested one-page failure on Codex: correct 247-word vectors content occupies two physical pages; creation was undone. This confirms the physical length limit is not confined to Spectra default.

- The fourth French card's saved explanation incorrectly says the first e takes the grave accent in “préfère”: the first remains é and the second is è. Its front/back spelling is correct; the first two rules are correct. This is persisted model output (`student-own-french-state.json`), separate from LV-36's missing explanation rendering. Do not rewrite the trashed historical result to disguise that quality limit.
- AI-047 automatically falls back to paid OpenRouter after DEVICE_STORE_UNAVAILABLE at 490 ms, before a Codex model event. This is the second fallback incident, not an intentionally paid request. Two saved account surfaces had recently rejoined after stack restart; simultaneous headless focus is only a hypothesis. The student performed ten activities and **18 completed run records**, with 19 user messages because one follow-up joined an active run. **17 runs stayed wholly on Codex GPT-6-Luna; one automatically fell back to paid OpenRouter after 120 seconds without activity.** Eighteen raw thread/settings/updated frames confirm gpt-6-luna / HIGH. Pooled Codex frames reside in AI-035's raw trace because the connection retains its initial async context; missing a per-run raw frame is not evidence of a different model. The structured run traces contain one failed calendar tool, followed by successful recovery; all other tools succeed. These facts do not mean every result met the request.

Complaint audit complete for the reported issues: missing-data School (LV-26), Calendar false postcondition/stale removed row (LV-27/28), selected pending progress (LV-34) and discarded flashcard explanations (LV-36) independently reproduce, are corrected and pass live checks. Current econ document is readable, with headings, separated steps, comparison table and graph embed; transient squashing/literal # does not recur on own reopening (`student-own-econ-01/02`, inspected), so no speculative editor change. This is an affordance issue for Pablo, not permission to bypass the sandbox. Own first-two-card flips and stored content expose the missing-rule renderer defect; source includes the rules. The historical deck remains readable after Trash, but is not resurrected: deletedAt stays 1794219841805, content updatedAt stays 1794219784454, one version (`student-own-french-state.json`). Panel clutter, cropped models, board fit, retained source panels and three-day responsive Calendar are recorded visual/design observations. Fictional Friday versus actual Monday is not a clock defect.

## Efficiency numbers

LV-61 replica-density correction: work metadata and both payload preferences previously read three rows at sparse density and 20 at dense density, with the dense response incorrectly empty/null. Library's existing own-attention range adds one row in both cases. The corrected reader ranges always return the two visible replicas, or three Library replica-table documents including attention, at either one or 100 foreign rows; full-handler calls/returned bytes are density-independent and writes are zero. Two cloud-present/absent cap cases retain the maximum 20 total replica rows. This needs two indexed ranges instead of one per affected reader, plus by_work_kind_account storage/maintenance on existing replica writes. Ordinary visible document bytes are equivalent; discarded private projections no longer cross the DB API boundary. This is a correctness/density tradeoff with explicit query/index overhead, not an unchanged-query-count claim or new aggregate idle/AI benchmark. No timer, external function call, subscription or UI is added.

LV-60 correctness cost: each validated harness answer now updates the existing canonical cards by run and their compact read models atomically. This adds a by_run collection plus per-card patches and existing projection-trigger maintenance; other harness mutations and idle paths are unchanged. Three controlled approval/rejection/question scenarios retain identical full-handler read-call/write counts at 1/100 unrelated runs/cards; the canonical-card range returns exactly one row in both, without filtering after its index. Duplicate answers write nothing. This is measured bounded consistency work, not a claimed cost saving or a new live aggregate benchmark. No schema/index/subscription/timer is added.

LV-59 stream/relay correction: three real paired-hook approval/rejection/question cases now perform zero extra getSpectraRunEvents reads instead of one after the answering engine finishes. No Convex function, query, index, timer or connection is added. One UUID (about 50 serialized bytes) is added to the existing scheduled payload per user dispatch. Automatic worker handoffs preserve its channel; prior paused and resumed buffers may coexist until the unchanged two-minute cleanup, with each channel bounded by the existing 2 MiB window. That transient memory/payload tradeoff is disclosed. Controlled tests and successful physical Word retests establish correctness; they do not replace the live idle/action windows below or establish a new aggregate billing reduction.

LV-58 native write identity: valid explicit pending/claimed receipts now use one validated point read instead of a latest-50 history query. Sparse/dense failed-history fixtures have identical handler calls/documents/writes after the fix and no request-history take. Four request-specific settlement ranges use the new resolutionRequestId/accountId/state index, selecting one target resolving row at either 1 or 100 unrelated same-account rows instead of up to 50 unrelated conflicts. Observed public Bridge handlers preserve full call/document counts between corrected densities, including trigger reads; the signed supersession path separately preserves state at both densities. One extra index entry per conflict adds storage/write maintenance. Normal lookup/query counts and 50-row limits remain. These controlled results do not update the earlier live idle/AI measurements or establish aggregate physical-I/O/billing savings.

LV-57 conflict resolution: the work-wide range previously selected/resolved 20 foreign conflicts and missed the own conflict beyond 100 peers. The corrected account range selects exactly one own conflict at either 1 or 100 peer rows; the observer also counts the existing trigger get, for two syncConflicts documents total at both densities. Total database calls and writes match across those corrected densities. Five production range edits reuse the existing index, add no query/index and retain original limits. Recording missing own conflicts now creates the intended own row; this correctness write is not described as a cost reduction. Aggregate physical I/O/billing is not measured here.

LV-56 account-density regression: with one own metadata row and either one or 100 foreign rows, work-wide post-filter reads previously grew from 2 to 20 conflicts or 2 to 50 operations and lost the own row. Account-bounded indexed ranges now read exactly one target-table row at both densities and retain exact own metadata. Query count stays constant, the handler writes nothing and original 20/50 ceilings remain. Two additional compound indexes add storage/index maintenance to existing table writes. This controlled result is separate from the live five-minute/AI measurements below; no aggregate billing or physical-I/O percentage is inferred.

- LV-51 removes payload-only run-index notifications, proven through the actual hook before/after. The complete **80-second** capture is **175 non-cron completions / 1,989,940 DB I/O bytes**, six gateway exchanges, zero local egress; its **10.292-second** active-UI interval has **121 / 1,891,897 bytes**. listTabs is 26 completions (six cached) / 443,396 bytes in both intervals. All usage fields are observed. The window contains new-thread hydration, title filing, both browsers' reactive data and a different plain-answer task from LV-50, so it establishes current costs without a causal aggregate reduction claim. Their combined disjoint active-UI intervals respectively have **238 / 2,370,747 bytes** and **217 / 1,846,003 bytes**. - **LV-21 reproduced / pre-edit consequence audit:** extending the actual Recent-work handler regression with a throwing clock fails exactly in `sharedByActor` at `orgTreeViews.ts:100`, called from `hydrateNodes` / `orgTree.ts:651` (`recent-work-sharing-before.log`, two neighbors pass). `hydrateNodes` uses its optional accountId only for outgoing invitation badges; Recent work discards that field. Remove only accountId from this caller's existing hydration options. Source ownership/status, account/live index, result shape, deduplication, all generic hydration callers, and the separate foreign-work capability/expiry check stay unchanged. The regression checks exact owned-work output with no wall-clock access, no invitation query and zero writes; the existing shared fixture checks permitted inclusion then exclusion on expiry. Finish the current index-only idle measurement and record a same-query focus-read sample before deploying this second change.

- Follow-up under investigation, not yet fixed: Recent work asks `hydrateNodes` for outbound-sharing badges by passing `accountId`, but returns only workId/title/typeKey/activityAt. In `orgTreeViews.ts`, that option is used solely by `sharedByActor` for invitation lookups and its wall clock; source ownership, status and the caller's explicit foreign-work permission check do not depend on the option. Removing only that unused request could avoid discarded queries and permit owner-path cache reuse. Before any edit, reproduce the actual handler with a throwing clock and check the complete response plus zero invitation queries; retain granted/expired shared-work tests. The active LV-20 full-suite/idle captures will finish without source changes first.

- Action-cost investigation: the bounded read-only Andy metadata audit finds 70 run tabs, all 70 on current projection version 3; 69 visible, one selected, no legacy projection and the 201-row bound is not reached (`audit-run-tab-projections.json`). `listTabs` already reads compact tab/card projections and falls back only for legacy data; no speculative backfill was run. `selectTab` currently reads all account tabs to clear selection and hide duplicate thread tabs; narrowing only its target query would omit required updates. A broader selected-tab/index or polling change needs its own behavioral/cost reproduction and is not justified by this index fix alone. The measured action-budget failure remains open.

- **LV-20 pre-edit consequence audit:** `orgTree:recentWorks` is called only by `RailRecentWorks` (one-off sidebar reads on navigation/focus and work/thread events); the other similarly named brief fields are unrelated data. Use the existing `by_account_live_kind_activity` index with `kind=work`, retaining account/live constraints, descending activity order, the 60-work bound, type exclusions, source hydration, deduplication and shared-access checks. This is one index-selection change, with no schema, UI, timer, cursor, new query or write. All work types/runtimes still flow through the same result shape and source permission checks. The existing inherited-access fixture now also checks recent-work inclusion and exclusion after grant expiry; the new density fixture checks duplicate placements, task exclusion, deleted sources, exact output and zero writes. The first test command rejected unsupported `--minWorkers` before running tests; rerunning with only the existing two-worker option produced the four expected assertions, with two neighboring tests passing.

### Earlier findings and pre-edit audits (historical)

The following entries preserve what was known before each edit. Their intermediate test counts, planned work and unresolved-at-that-time statements are historical; use Summary, Fixed and Found, not fixed for current status.

- The Devices confirmation follow-up now reproduces all three old-account continuations (`devices-transition-before-final.log`, 3 failed / 10 passed): late revoke starts native unpair, late unpair calls `forgetSession`, and late native-write policy calls the preferences updater. The first diagnostic's policy selector missed the accessible name; correcting only that selector produced the clean third failure. Add the existing synchronous revision checks at handler entry and after the awaited operation before its next side effect, with stale catch/finally UI updates ignored. No live device removal or settings mutation was performed.

- **LV-05 Devices confirmation follow-up audit (before any production edit):** source review found `DevicesPage.confirmAction` awaits cloud revocation before calling the current browser's `unpairBridge`, then awaits it before `forgetSession`; `applyNativeWrites` awaits a policy mutation before calling the preferences updater. Both are outer continuations of the same account-transition class. Reproduce by holding each await in the existing rendered component test, unmounting and using real storage cleanup to switch to B; no real revoke, pairing or settings action is permitted. The DevicesPage, local control/session callers, native-write plan and preference updater were searched. If confirmed, check the existing originating account revision before the next side effect and ignore old completion/errors, preserving the ordinary confirmation behavior, visuals and database call count. The current completed full-suite checkpoint precedes this pending investigation.

- **Idle-query ancestor hypothesis (resolved without a product edit):** caller/source search covered Home/Library quick views, all breadcrumb users and the largest logged idle query. Its loop appeared to request the same parent for each sibling. An actual-handler observer with one versus twelve siblings disproved extra database gets because the existing function builder's `withReadOnce` already coalesces them. Paths, ordering and zero writes passed. No authentication, subscription or schema change was justified, and no production efficiency edit was made for this hypothesis.

- **LV-05 build-import consequence audit (before correction):** the actual Home reload returns a Next.js build error because the new Bridge client import of `account-storage.ts` reaches React's `useSyncExternalStore` through the browser run ledger (`bridge-transition-build-regression-before.png`, inspected). The server's pure Devices model also imports this shared client module. Keep the existing browser cleanup and its API intact, but put only the account scope/revision state and synchronous checks in a dependency-free module shared by both environments. Search covered every account-storage caller, the Devices model, server harness import trace and Hocuspocus transport; do not mark the whole client as a Client Component or change server/runtime behavior. Repeat real Home/Devices reload and focused/full tests after this correction. TypeScript alone did not catch this bundler regression.

- **LV-05 room-setup continuation audit (before editing):** a real Hocuspocus transport is destroyed while its cloud session is pending, then the real account cleanup switches to B. The late A payload still calls B's native summary and seed before the caller's destroyed check (`bridge-transition-room-before.log`, two native requests). Fence the shared session fetch before native entry and include the account revision in its in-flight key, preserving same-account coalescing, deadlines and the existing transport lifecycle. Final projection may still save through its already-bound native socket, but must not send a cloud metadata touch under another account. The adjacent manual move/backup/restore UI callers also have outer query continuations; they must check the same revision before entering a new inner operation and avoid publishing old-account completion/errors.

- **LV-05 browser transition correction audit (before production edits):** real storage-boundary regressions reproduce stale shared status, a late resumed/pairing session crossing accounts, stale work JSON/store ids and a backup upload after the account changed (`bridge-transition-before-all.log`). Trace/caller searches cover the account boundary, all protected Bridge body readers, pairing factories, store moves/seed/room setup, background store publication and backup/restore callers. Reuse the existing account/deployment storage scope plus an in-memory revision retired at owner change, including the identity-loading gap; scope in-memory caches and refuse stale continuations before persisting a token, publishing private data or starting the next write/upload. Preserve same-account auth-refresh continuity, offline editing, probe sharing/backoff and request deadlines. This needs several shared seams because fixing only localStorage or only a React disposed flag leaves the other reproduced races. No native protocol, UI layout, cloud capability, query or subscription is added.

- **Withdrawn browser-boundary inference:** the earlier disposable native probe retained and replayed a valid A bearer token (`browser-session-before.log`). It did not invoke the real application's account storage boundary, which already clears `spectra.bridge.*` and unmounts private readers. Its diagnostic false result is not proof that normal account switching preserves that token, and is not a failing release gate. The previous claim that a new cloud capability was required was unsupported and has been removed. Real cleanup regressions then reproduced the narrower late-response/cache defects now fixed above. No cloud capability or offline authorization dependency was introduced.

Earlier native persistence checkpoint, before production binding: all 141 core tests pass (`account-store-core-final.log`, 2.99 s), and Bridge test compilation passes (`account-store-app-final.log`). **LV-05 production binding consequence audit (before editing):** searched ServerState pairing/revocation, all four shared-room lookups, pair/resume/health callers, startup restoration and hosted pipe lifetimes. Bind rooms to the verified account/deployment and pairing generation, reuse one Rooms instance per account during A→B→A switches to avoid overlapping close/compaction, and refresh/clear restore credentials without dropping that room cache. Pairing/health supply ownership from their existing authenticated response; initial migration runs only after the fixed listener is acquired, preserves the source and fails closed. Hosted child pipes must close on pairing changes and must abort their CLI task on cancellation; no additional polling or ordinary edit-time ownership query is introduced. Disposable tests cover these paths; the installed Bridge is not run or changed. Browser-origin account authorization remains a separate open boundary.

- **LV-05 account-store/migration implementation audit (before editing):** traced WorkStore constructors, every SQL table, room initialization, versions, backup/restore and the four shared-store entry-point lookups. Add an account/deployment-derived private store while preserving the normal legacy file. Copy only the explicit ids returned by the verified-owner lookup, including their exact state, update log, FTS row and history ids, in one transaction; skip an already-present scoped work, preserve the legacy store id and reset backup coverage. Keep the migration-complete marker separate so a failed/incomplete copy cannot be published as an empty native room. The source is attached read-only with a file URI (`mode=ro`); [SQLite URI rules](https://www.sqlite.org/uri.html) and [rusqlite 0.37 default flags](https://docs.rs/rusqlite/0.37.0/rusqlite/struct.OpenFlags.html) confirm that the existing connection enables URI ATTACH handling. This is necessary persistence work for the reproduced re-pairing leak, not a general storage redesign. Disposable tests must prove account/deployment separation, legacy null-owner filtering from verified ids, exact history/state preservation, unchanged source, retry/rollback and preservation of newer scoped work before wiring the live entry points. No installed work database is opened by these tests.

Before the restore edit: a scoped store must refuse an unverified restore; parse its backup ids, prove them through the existing bounded cloud lookup, then restore only that set. Reuse one parser for validation and restoration so malformed/truncated data cannot disagree between passes. Normal editing and legacy unscoped tests must retain their behavior; only explicit restore incurs this proof/validation work.

- **LV-05 retained-work source audit (stored-work layer not edited):** `bridge-core/src/works.rs` already stores an optional `owner` per work, but only search accepts an optional owner filter; `Rooms::call` forwards get/getMany/state/version/remove/backup/restore without an authenticated account boundary. Unpairing retains this store, so checking a device id alone would not safely handle re-pairing to another account. Browser sessions are keyed by origin. Migration must preserve existing work and versions, including legacy rows without an owner, rather than claiming all old rows for whichever account pairs next. These are source findings extending the reproduced AI filename leak, not claims of additional live probes. Pablo's installed Bridge and data have not been changed.
  **Retained-work behavior now reproduced in a disposable server:** the external native fixture saves two dummy works through the real protected `/v1/works/call` route, one with a first-account owner and one with no owner, then forgets pairing, pairs with a different device identity and reads both through that new browser session. Both are exposed (`foreignOwnedHidden: false`, `legacyUnclaimedHidden: false`; `store-repair-before.log`). The fixture asserts the absolute work-store override before starting, uses a random localhost port, never calls relay-token preparation or a CLI, and touches only its new disposable profile. Its expected isolation assertion fails; this is an intentionally failing diagnostic outside the release test suite, whose 130 native tests still pass. This establishes a separate retained-work defect after the original filename-transport correction, without touching Pablo's installed Bridge or real data.
  **Larger-fix direction before production edits:** all store/room/hub entry points must select a store bound to a cloud-verified account, with ongoing connections holding that binding. Four current `rooms::shared()` lookups serve these HTTP/socket/hub paths. A private store per account/deployment avoids adding authorization lookups to each Yjs update and keeps normal database/network cost unchanged. Pair/resume/health already authenticate the device and can return its owner without an extra read. Legacy rows cannot be assigned from their optional local `owner` or from the next pairing alone: discover legacy ids, verify their ownership in bounded credential-authenticated cloud reads, then copy only proven rows, updates and versions without deleting the old database or overwriting newer scoped work. Include the old normal-store fallback for profiles affected by the override bug. `spectraArtifactSummaries` already has ownerAccountId and an indexed by_artifact lookup, offering a compact ownership authority instead of reading normal whole artifact content. Preserve the old store id for already-referenced migrated work; reset backup coverage rather than inheriting another account's global last-backup state. An incomplete migration must not publish an empty store that the browser could seed over local-only work. Browser sessions also require an authenticated account boundary rather than a client-claimed account id. This remains engineering work, not a request to bypass the blocker with the installed Bridge or call LV-05 complete.
  Isolation setup limitation: the work-store override defect has now been corrected under Fixed, but the loopback listener and relay token remain shared by the installation. Rebuilt live testing must therefore use a separately isolated machine/VM or OS environment with its own local app data and listener, rather than launching a second Bridge beside Pablo's real one. The current Rust network fixture uses an ephemeral listener and in-memory store, so it does not have this exposure.

  Pre-edit cause/caller audit: `use-work-previews.ts`'s `faceBlock`, used only by resource Word card previews, treats RichBlock.raw as text. ProseMirror-derived rich AST deliberately uses serialized JSON as raw semantic identity (`identifyBlocks`); the shared `richContentToPlainText` already extracts paragraph/heading/list words, links, math and revision visibility correctly. Use that existing semantic serializer at this boundary, preserving literal #/_/* characters rather than stripping Markdown from structured data. Do not change AST identity, document import, native writes, other artifact previews or the cloud. Existing persisted bad Word faces need a targeted cache-URL format revision; leave other preview keys and the account-cleared/bounded cache namespace unchanged, with no added reads after the corrected face is cached. Test both fresh structured AST and a pre-existing bad persisted face through the actual public preview hook. The separate first-open promotion is explicitly `replicas.activateLens`; do not remove its payload/type/concurrency checks or confuse that conversion with a preview rendering fix.

- **LV-22 web-only first connection and automatic account rejoin — reproduced, source audit in progress.** Latest user direction requires connection entirely through the web, quick recognition/reconnection on account changes, and low-level computer use only when native file/folder selection is necessary. The fresh verification Bridge required a separate native code confirmation before Andy connected (`native-04` / `native-06` and the native dialog). In the saved pmartinez account, even explicit Connect fails with “current connection cannot be used with this account” and tells the user to disconnect in the tray and choose files again (`native-switch-01` / `02`, inspected). `window.Clerk.user` was read only for the whitelisted public email/name, confirming pmartinez@student-tgc.com / P MD; no password, cookie, bearer or key value was read.
  Root paths: `BridgeConnection` only auto-rejoins when public health says already paired; `complete_pairing_offer` explicitly invokes a native confirmation on the first connection and refuses an account-mismatched saved credential instead of completing the new account's authenticated web challenge. Pre-edit consequence audit: its only caller is `SpectraLocalControl::auto_pair`; resume/confirm cloud ownership, challenge expiry and installation checks remain unchanged. On account change, retire old PairingWork leases before changing the journal, rotate browser sessions and relay generation, clear active grants without deleting persisted permissions/files, then restore only grants bound to the confirmed device. Reuse the existing account/deployment-scoped work store. This affects native watchers, local store/room/CLI sockets, startup session restoration, tray and Devices; test direct A→B→A transitions, same-account continuity and cancellation fences. Factor only the existing grant restore path needed by both startup and switching. The new-account confirm exchange obtains that account's own credential; an old account's credential is never accepted for a different owner. ### LV-61 visible-replica density investigation / audit BEFORE production edit

Previous goal turn is progress: LV-59/60 close two reproduced approval-resume causes and the transient enabled-action state, with physical OpenRouter/Codex writes, exact Undo, 160 focused tests and the source-frozen terminal 5,742-test whole/static/integrity gate. This investigation concerns the separately disclosed replica-set density boundary; it does not repeat passing model runs.

The three read paths forWork, presentationsForAccount and payloadForWork take the first 20 work-wide replicas before retaining shared cloud rows and the authenticated account's own desktop rows. Other accounts' older local copies can consume that limit, making this account's available file/payload disappear and reading discarded full projection documents. Reproduce all three actual handlers (both cloud and local-latest payload preference) with 100 foreign local rows before a valid shared cloud/own local pair, then the identical pair with one foreign row. Require identical correct results, zero writes and density-independent replica rows/serialized bytes. Use only isolated Convex-test fixtures, not real foreign user data.

Caller audit covers ArtifactStudio, ResourceSurface, FileStorageControls, Library replica presentations, native restore/Yjs AI operation, DOCX/Office routes, harness native effects and native-payload-server's verified fetch/import callers. They select cloud/local rows by kind, ID or predicates; preserve chronological row order and the 20-row returned/read ceiling. Artifact capability checks, other-device availability, conflicts/operations, protected pending selection, payload ownership/state/hash verification and native approval/write admission stay unchanged. Writers and global sweep/migration paths are audited separately; correcting these readers must not silently change producer account authority.

If reproduced, one narrow work/kind/account index and one small shared routine inside the existing replicas.ts will replace these three identical read/filter paths. The routine reads the canonical cloud row through the existing work/kind range and only this account's local rows through the new bound, with at most 20 returned DB rows combined, sorted by creation time. Existing cloud producers treat cloud truth as one canonical row (unique/first); retain the existing first cloud choice. This needs two DB ranges instead of one per affected read, plus one index entry's storage/maintenance per workReplicas write; it is a correctness/privacy-density tradeoff, not an unchanged-query-count claim. Ordinary visible document bytes must stay equivalent, while foreign projections cease crossing the DB API boundary. No new file/table/cache, polling, UI change, unbounded scan or schema redesign is proposed.

LV-61 actual-handler reproduction: all four new cases fail with 80 existing passes (replica-visible-61-before-final.log). Dense rows hide both visible replicas, empty the Library replica list/pending count, and return null for both verified payload preferences. Work/payload replica reads grow from three rows to 20; the payload examples range from 12,146–12,149 sparse serialized bytes to 217,233–217,238 dense bytes (fixture IDs/timestamps account for small differences). Library reads four versus 21 replica-table documents including its separate own-attention row (12,858 versus 217,954 bytes). These are DB API returned rows/UTF-8 JSON bytes, not physical I/O or billing. The first run stops later assertions at the call-count difference (device/payload follow-up lookups disappear with the hidden replicas); the preserved second run uses soft assertions to record all independent failures without weakening the final expected result/counts. Add cloud-present/absent cap checks before the production correction, then verify the same valid fixtures and neighboring native/security callers.


### LV-61 final verification closure (10 November)

Four actual-handler density failures and two cap cases pass after a shared bounded visible-replica read replaces three work-wide post-filter reads. The new index excludes foreign local projections while preserving shared cloud truth; one additional range and index storage/maintenance are explicitly disclosed. All 280 focused tests and the terminal 340.93-second whole gate (665 files / 5,748 tests, one existing skip) pass, plus both TypeScript projects, zero-warning lint and whitespace. All 175 source/config hashes/set match across the gate; only the audited query/test/schema trio differs from LV-60.

Deployment, automatic original-profile Bridge connection, identical native text/Saved, exact original Word/Vectors/task snapshots, physical hashes, rebuilt binary and all four preference layers pass. Retained work opens for 77.476 seconds with exact before/after artifact/version/queried-sync snapshots; both browser contexts are healthy and all ten new screenshots were inspected. No AI, native reset/reconfiguration, stack restart or commit/push/PR is needed. This goal turn is concrete reliability and bounded-read progress. Historical model/quiet-turn/fallback, other producer/density caveats and strict aggregate budgets remain disclosed; no blanket perfection/launch-completion claim is made.
